## 📅 2024-10-01

**[17:54] Levent：** Hi Simon, how are you ? Sorry to bother you on your holidays...
Do you have the following certifications for the industrial product
EN61000-4-2(ESD),EN61000-4-4 (EFT),EN61000-4-5,IEC 60068-2-27,IEC 60068-2-32,IEC 60068-2-6
EMC Standards
The EMC standards you mentioned are not covered by EN 62368-1:2020:
EN 61000-4-2 (ESD -Electrostatic Discharge)
EN 61000-4-4 (EFT - Electrical Fast Transient)
EN 61000-4-5 (Surge)
These standards are part of the IEC 61000 series, which deals with electromagnetic compatibility testing3.
They are typically required separately for EMC compliance and CE marking.
Environmental Testing Standards
The IEC 60068 series standards you listed are also not covered by EN 62368-1:2020:
IEC 60068-2-27 (Shock)
IEC 60068-2-32 (Free fall)
IEC 60068-2-6 (Vibration)
These standards are part of the IEC 60068 series, which focuses on environmental testing of electronic and
electricalproducts4.

thanks for your info

## 📅 2025-12-03

**[14:50] Simon：** Good morning, Levent,
1, MTBF: this value can be just modified, no big issue
2, RAM and Flash: it can be 2GB each.
RAM: we can simply change it to 2GB without modifying the PCBs
Flash: we need to adapt the PCBs to make it 2GB
So you can just say, 2GB each is fine.
If we win this tender, we will just make it 2GB each in production.

**[14:50] Simon：** 3, Big problem about IP SLA
According to our engineering team, this is a Cisco-based function.
It includes different small features like ICMP echo, ICMP jitter, ftp etc.
ICMP echo can be replaced by Ping/Tracert
Well, a big problem about ICMP jitter, it involves software, we cannot give a definite conclusion now
We can only say, trying might work
And about Jitter
I talked with GPT, reply is there are 3 types of Jitter
Three Types of Jitter Implementations
1. Basic RTT Jitter (Simple Consecutive RTT Difference)
This method calculates jitter based on the difference between consecutive ICMP Echo RTT measurements.
Send continuous ICMP Echo requests
Record each RTT value
Jitter = (RTT(n) - RTT(n-1)I (average or maximum)
No timestamp field required
No support needed from the remote device
Y Easiest to implement, widely accepted in most RFPs.
2. ICMP Timestamp-Based Jitter (Enhanced Payload Timestamp)
This method adds a custom timestamp field into the ICMP Echo payload.
Insert a sender-side timestamp into the ICMP payload
Remote device simply echoes back the payload
Use RTT and timestamp to compute jitter
Does not require any special support on the remote device
V Still easy to implement; suitable for professional-grade jitter measurement.
3. Cisco-Style UDP Jitter (Full IP SLA Responder)
This method mimics Cisco IP SLA Jitter using UDP packets with timestamp information.
Requires both sender and responder to support the protocol
Parses timestamps for one-way delay and jitter
Measures packet loss, MOS, and detailed statistics
This is Cisco proprietary and not supported by most vendors
x High implementation complexity, not recommended unless explicitly required.
Accroding to GPT, it should be about type 1 or type 2
not very difficult
anyway, please confirm with Taylan or your client about Jitter type
4, SFTP is OK
We discussed this last year, it is ready now.
pls let me know, thanks

**[13:43] Simon：** Hi, Levent,
1. About shipment
Express is NOT an option, it is too expensive.
We will only consider sea shipment
Cost is 50K~70K RMB, I am still waiting for info...
According to this shipping forwarder, it seems to be DDP
This sounds good because it includes import duty and customs
But, there is also risk in this
Let me explain how this works
Those shipping forwarders got many registered companies around the world
Mostly shell companies, but it is legally fine
They will act as the importer, their info will show on the tracking document
they will do the customs clearance, pay the import duty etc.
Once materials received, it will be shipped to final and real buyers like Angora
This is not rare in foreign trader, well, it is kinda frequent actually
We did it many times for various clients
The importer on the tracking info will be a local shell company registered by the shipping forwarder
this may cause 2 main problems
One is the import itself, this is a big project, total value is huge
It is really worrying if something is wrong in import.
Typically, it will be just fine, BUT, it is still a potential risk!!
The other is the info on the tracking document
you are handing the materials over to the police and get payment AFTER approval
So it will be, will they demand exactly the same info between bid winner(Angora) and real importer(a local

**[14:41] Levent：** ## The key structural observations from the data:
1. Types 3, 4, 5 are nearly identical in features — the only differences are that Type 4 has no stacking
requirement, and Type 5 adds the industrial temperature/fanless constraint.

the Add-on variant in Type 2.
3. Type 6 is the outlier — fiber-only ports, much higher performance (128 Mpps / 196 Gbps), adds BGP,
drops STP/RSTP/MSTP, and requires redundant PSU. It also does not explicitly require 802.1X or TACACS+.
